Shared process ownership belongs in `umpire-backend-process`, separate from TLA+ emission. A host-only `xtask` replaces build helpers, their embedded Python glue and controls. The retained-original Python hash verifier is retired, not ported; the Rust lifecycle comparison stays and no retained artifact changes. Go stays where the host requires it; Bash remains orchestration.\n\nDevelopers use documented native Rust driver contracts, with callers updated together. Keep cheap useful happy-path invocations; no aliases, diagnostic spellings or old encodings are required solely for backward compatibility. The post-fn-237.3 integrated guest is semantic reference evidence, not a byte oracle. This work changes no Model, checker meaning, protocol, IR or Temporal feature behavior.\n\n## Architecture & Data Models\n\n`umpire-backend-process` owns the existing `Limits`, `Refusal`, `Scratch`, `ProcessFailure`, Java admission, process execution and bounded-text contracts. TLA+, TLC and Apalache use it directly where they need those contracts; Apalache acquires no TLC dependency. TLA+ retains binding, emission, Snowcat and typed-value admission. Update callers coherently where the native interface differs; preserve functional typed failure/status mapping and all process safety controls. Old signatures, compatibility reexports and diagnostic wording are not acceptance criteria solely for backward compatibility.\n\nUse an allocation-free process supervisor inside the process crate. Prepare executable resolution, argument/environment bytes, descriptors and bounded buffers before forking. Every path in the forked supervisor and its pre-exec target child is restricted to audited libc calls and stack-only scalar/byte operations; it cannot allocate, format, lock, run Rust destructors, unwind or call general `std` process/filesystem helpers. All child exits use `_exit`. This is the production path for focused Cargo tests and library consumers; no sibling executable, runtime Cargo invocation or warm workspace build is required. A standalone Rust helper is an alternative only if a reviewed build/discovery contract first proves all those use cases.\n\nLinux retains the private supervisor group, target session, subreaper and adopted-descendant cleanup, including escaped process groups. Darwin retains non-root admission and descendant prevention. Other platforms refuse at admission. Signals and owner-liveness EOF preserve the existing outcome mapping. Safe Rust retains capture handles, inode checks, output concatenation, dump admission and scratch cleanup. After the supervisor proof, scratch directories use `tempfile::Builder` and parent-side syscalls use `rustix` safe wrappers; the forked child's async-signal-safe path stays raw libc. Limits keep their existing units and enforcement: Java heap configuration is not an OS memory cap, and polling a regular output file is not a strict kernel output cap.\n\n`xtask` is a native development-profile binary, excluded from guest/wasm dependency closures. Its commands use documented native Rust arguments, JSON schemas, status/error rendering and TOML behavior; retain useful ordinary invocations without emulating Python quirks. It hashes in-process with `sha2` (no `shasum` subprocess), encodes JSON with plain `serde_json` (default BTreeMap-backed `Map`; `preserve_order` off workspace-wide), and handles OS arguments with `std::env::args_os`/`OsString` (no clap), decoding command tokens only where needed. Internal commands replace cache-key calculation, launcher publication and Cargo-artifact selection formerly embedded in the guest shell. Compile xtask before setting guest wrapper/encoded flags; the compiler wrapper directly executes the built native tool, never recursively starts Cargo. Preserve raw compiler argv, previous-wrapper execution, jobserver descriptors and macOS loader environment.\n\n## API Contracts\n\nPreserve `run_process` and cancellable execution's distinction between refusal, child status and retained output. Preserve timeout/cancellation output, first-signal handling, owner EOF, target signal mapping, `125` owner-loss status and `128+signal` outcomes as applicable. Diagnostic text may follow the native interface; callers must still distinguish typed refusals, launch failures and child outcomes.\n\nGuest identities, hash inputs and sidecar records use native Rust schemas and remain deterministic, portable and stable going forward. Invalidate stale caches when encoding changes instead of supporting old formats. Sidecars publish before compilation and disappear on failure. Closure identity retains checksums, non-dev edges across all platforms, build dependencies, edge structure, ambiguity and shared-feature unification behavior.\n\nGuest check mode distinguishes fresh, stale/missing and tool/build/discovery failure, reports useful differences and never publishes the candidate. Ordinary useful status values may remain; old Python formatting/first-offset rendering is not required. Missing prerequisites and probe/metadata/build/staleness failures are explicit failures under the native driver contract, regardless of CI presence. This helper policy change does not change R5 backend default/skip behavior.\n\nRust/Cargo and the documented mise toolchain are prerequisites. Freshness enters through a minimal Bash bootstrap launcher that sets `MISE_AUTO_INSTALL=false` and `MISE_EXEC_AUTO_INSTALL=false` before its first probe. Missing bootstrap tools and bootstrap compilation errors fail clearly without guest build or publication. Bootstrap xtask before guest wrapper/flags are installed; Rust then owns authoritative TOML/JSON parsing, installed-tool metadata, pin consistency, Rust/rustup/Go/protoc/rust-src/wasm readiness and freshness validation. Do not duplicate semantic validators in Bash or retain the old local/CI missing-tool skip matrix. An existing cache must not bypass native validation. Prove the actual cold Make/shell entrypoint, disabled auto-install from the first probe, fail-fast/compiler-failure behavior and never-publish check mode.\n\nThe retained-original hash integrity check is thrown away (owner, 2026-10-24): `verify.py` is removed, not ported, and `manifest.json` (paths and hashes) is deleted. The originals stay in Git as historical evidence; the completed fn-227 checksum artifact under `.flow/artifacts` is history and is not consulted. The compatibility checkpoint keeps its semantic lifecycle comparison against `original/rows.snap.txt` and its mutation control.\n\n## Edge Cases & Constraints\n\nExecution admission requires fn-237.3 done, with its implementation and Flow closure commits present in `umpire` ancestry. Begin from that integrated tip and record its guest hash as reference evidence before changing tools. A native ready result alone does not clear this gate. Do not add a whole-fn-237 dependency: its later tasks follow this spec and fn-246. Preserve fn-246's whole-spec dependency on this spec. The separate fn-246/whole-fn-243 scheduling correction belongs to that plan.\n\nPromote `libc` 0.2.191 and, under the conductor's explicit autonomous R18 amendment, the already resolved `toml` 1.1.8+spec-1.1.0 for host-only xtask parsing. The latter retains checksum `20489e00e4d8741d6be680764cc12e270655e375a20d1011e844a9c3379e678d`, disables default features and requests only existing `std`, `parse` and `serde` features. New dependencies are admitted under fn-210 R18 (a release within 12 months, or finished and very widely used). Admitted here: `tar` 0.4.46 (default features off, no xattr) and `flate2` 1.1.10 (miniz_oxide backend) for the installer; `sha2` 0.11 for xtask hashing; `tempfile` 3.27 and `rustix` 1.1 (already resolved) for the process crate. Record before/after package and feature inventories, including the guest closure and each new transitive package's R18 basis; host-only additions never enter a guest closure, and `cargo deny check bans` passes. Keep workspace `unsafe_code=forbid`. The process crate alone may follow the existing runtime precedent: mirror the workspace lint policy with crate-local `unsafe_code=deny`, allowing unsafe only in its audited syscall module. All other modules forbid unsafe; no workspace-wide relaxation is allowed. Reuse safe standard-library facilities where their behavior is equivalent and use syscall wrappers only where required.\n\nFull TOML parsing uses native `toml` 1.1.8+spec-1.1.0 with its approved `std`/`parse`/`serde` features, grammar, limits and errors. Current Cargo/mise/rust-toolchain/rust-src documents must work. No `unbounded` feature, Python compatibility parser, BigInt emulation, Python recursion/digit/date/NaN/Unicode quirks or diagnostic-order emulation is required. Native serde_json schemas may replace Python encodings; document intentional driver breaks and update callers/tests together. No system tar, member filtering, verbose listing, GNU/BSD parity or platform receipts. Earlier process/guest work continues independently.\n\nAcceptance checks semantic correctness, safety and deterministic current-input reproduction, not old/new byte or diagnostic equality. Review any necessary guest/golden/pin regeneration, document its cause and old/new hashes, and verify reproducibility plus freshness against the newly committed asset. Prefer one integrated regeneration; repeat only for changed inputs or a specific failed proof. Never use normalization or repinning to hide semantic regressions or weaken mutation/negative controls. Runtime/PID/path/compiler/curl output is checked structurally.
